## Backend Slice 2 · B2-1 (2026-08-06 — identity & authorization schema foundation, awaiting owner review)

Implemented per approved docs/26 §8 (Amendment A1) on the Slice-1 conventions. **Schema and tests only — no identity services, no Cognito integration or cloud resource, no routes, no middleware, no CLI.**

**Migration `0002_identity.sql`** (one transaction, reviewed Down, no PG-18-only features): `app_user` (status/lock semantics, mfa_enrolled mirror flag) · `auth_identity` (normalized **issuer + subject** uniqueness — never email; per-user composite unique as the session FK target; partial unique on **verified** lower(email) so duplicate emails never merge users; `is_private_relay` representable without special cases; **no password/credential columns**) · `customer_account` (one per user) · `participant` foundation (kind self|child, **one-self partial unique invariant**; no user/login linkage — participants are never principals; full child model + counsel fields deferred to Slice 5) · `login_session` (issuer/subject/`origin_jti` provider references only — **no token material**; composite FK ties the session's identity to the same user; one **live** session per `origin_jti`; device/inventory fields; retention-class comments, no invented periods) · `auth_challenge` bookkeeping (no codes/secrets) · `admin_role_assignment` (five roles, **no super-admin value**; `requested_by ≠ approved_by` CHECK; finance-capable active requires approver; per-(user,role) active partial unique; **transition-guard trigger** requested→active|denied, active→revoked|expired; **D4 exclusivity trigger** — access_admin ∦ finance, auditor ∦ mutating roles — serialized per user via advisory xact lock; approval modeled as fields on the assignment row per docs/26 §8.7; platform-engineer separation deliberately NOT fabricated in PostgreSQL) · `bootstrap_seal` (singleton PK + CHECK, `forbid_mutation` trigger — exactly one successful production bootstrap ever; CLI itself is B2-5) · himma_app grants (SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE on identity tables, **no DELETE anywhere**, SELECT-only on the seal). Kysely types regenerated from the live schema (12 tables; deterministic re-run proven). `db:verify` extended with per-migration object checks (`SCHEMA_CHECKS` map in [migrations.ts](backend/src/db/migrations.ts)).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **70/70 across 8 suites** (29 new) on real PostgreSQL — migrate-from-zero incl. 0002; down/up round-trip (latest-migration policy); issuer+subject uniqueness incl. cross-issuer; unverified-duplicate emails coexist with **zero merging** while verified duplicates are refused; one account per user; one self participant per account; child-login structural impossibility; **structural secret-column scan** (no password/secret/token/totp/recovery/credential column in any public table; no refresh/token table exists); session ownership composite-FK denial, live-`origin_jti` uniqueness with post-revocation reuse, version-CAS revoke-once; dual-control CHECKs incl. concurrent approval race (exactly one winner via version CAS); invalid role transitions rejected; both exclusivity pairs both directions **incl. concurrent conflicting activation** (advisory-lock serialization, exactly one active); super-admin value rejected; bootstrap seal single-row + immutable even for the owner; himma_app permission matrix; audit+outbox atomic with identity mutations (commit and rollback both proven) · Slice-1 suites green (two tests updated to be migration-count-agnostic: down-test targets the latest migration; broken-migration fixture renamed `9999_broken.sql`) · codegen drift check clean · root non-regression **from the repo root under Node 22**: tsc clean, ESLint clean, frontend Jest **421/421**. Expo Doctor not re-run (no root config change this task; the previously recorded SDK-57 registry drift stands as a separate `chore(deps)` item).

### B2-1 correction (2026-08-06 — owner conditional approval of `8ac5065`; migration 0003 at `7565766`, **fully approved with B2-1 the same day**)

The owner conditionally approved B2-1 and directed one narrowly scoped schema correction covering exactly two guarantees. Applied as migration `0003_email_ownership_and_finance_approver_controls.sql`; no identity-model redesign, no other scope.

**Guarantee 1 — verified-email ownership is per USER, not per identity row.** The 0002 global partial unique index on verified `lower(email)` wrongly blocked one user from linking two identities (e.g. Apple + Google) that legitimately verify the same email. Replaced by an **exclusion constraint** `excl_auth_identity_verified_email_owner EXCLUDE USING gist ((lower(email)) WITH =, user_id WITH <>) WHERE (email_verified AND status='active')` on `btree_gist` (trusted core extension, adds no tables). Rows conflict only when the normalized email matches AND the owning user differs, so: same user may hold several active identities verifying one email · two users can never both actively verify one email · unverified/ended duplicates coexist and never merge anything · enforcement is the index machinery itself, so concurrent cross-user first-login/linking claims admit exactly one owner with no trigger or lock · issuer+subject matching (0002 constraints) untouched — email still never identifies an identity.

**Guarantee 2 — finance-capable activation requires two qualified Access Administrators.** New **deferred constraint trigger** `enforce_finance_activation_controls` (checked at commit, re-reading the row's final state): activation of `finance`/`access_admin` requires `requested_by ≠ approved_by` and BOTH holding a currently `active`, unexpired (`expires_at` checked against `now()`, not just the sweep) `access_admin` assignment that **predates the activating transaction** (`age(xmin) > 0` discipline) — so `requested`/`denied`/`revoked`/time-expired assignments never qualify and two conspirators cannot self-qualify by inserting a cross-referenced pair in one transaction. **Bootstrap preserved with no bypass branch for finance:** the single transaction that inserts the `bootstrap_seal` row may qualify same-transaction `access_admin` rows (the docs/26 §9.9 cross-witnessed pair validates itself at commit); the seal admits one row ever (singleton + `forbid_mutation`) and `himma_app` can only SELECT it, so the path exists once, for the elevated bootstrap CLI, and structurally cannot create a `finance` role. **Concurrency:** the check takes the 0002 per-user advisory xact-lock keys on requester and approver (deterministic order); new trigger `serialize_access_admin_release` takes the same lock whenever an `access_admin` row leaves `active`, so approval-vs-revocation resolves to exactly one serial order (an approval committing after a concurrent revocation re-reads the revoked state and fails). New trigger `enforce_admin_role_immutability` closes post-activation rewriting: `user_id`/`role`/`requested_by` immutable, witnesses (`approved_by`/`denied_by`/`revoked_by`) immutable once set, `expires_at` frozen after `requested` — the rule binds every writer including `himma_app`; no service-layer path can bypass it. `db:verify` SCHEMA_CHECKS extended with `constraint`/`extension` kinds and the 0003 objects.

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **86/86 across 9 suites** (16 new/updated) on real PostgreSQL, each suite migrating a blank database from zero through 0003 · new `finance-approver-controls` suite: conspiracy cross-pair without the seal refused with nothing committed · rogue bootstrap attempting `finance` refused with the seal unconsumed · approved bootstrap shape succeeds (fixture `bootstrapAccessAdmins`, the test-side §9.9 equivalent) · two valid distinct access_admins activate finance · unqualified requester refused · unqualified approver refused · `requested`/`denied` disqualified · `revoked` disqualified · time-expired-but-unswept disqualified · requester=approver still refused by the 0002 CHECK · `himma_app` itself is bound by the trigger · role-mutation bypass (active `operations` → `finance`) refused · **deterministic concurrent approval-vs-revocation**: an approval commit blocks on the in-flight revocation's advisory lock (observed blocked, then refused after the revocation commits; final state = revoked admin, request still `requested`) · witness/identity-column immutability · identity-schema additions: one user links two active identities sharing a verified email · concurrent cross-user claims of one verified email admit exactly one owner with both users intact (nothing merged) · unverified-duplicate and cross-user-verified-refusal tests from 0002 still green over the new constraint · admin-roles fixtures rebased on the bootstrap pair (seal tests now assert the fixture's singleton) · down/up round-trip on 0003 green (reviewed Down restores the 0002 index; documented to fail if same-user duplicates exist — dev/test only) · dev `db:migrate` applied 0003; `db:verify` passes · Kysely codegen re-run deterministic with **zero drift** (no column changes) · root untouched: no repository-level configuration changed, so root frontend and native certification are not re-run per the owner directive.

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-2 (2026-08-06 — provider-independent identity core & adapter foundations, this commit, awaiting owner review)

Implemented per docs/26 §13 B2-2 (§9.1–9.3 services, adapter port + fake + Cognito adapter, mail port with dev capture) on the approved B2-1/0003 schema. **No HTTP routes, no middleware, no login sessions (B2-3 seam), no admin-role services, no MFA, no cloud resource, no email vendor, no Apple/Google configuration.**

**Modules introduced (`backend/src/modules/identity/`):** `providers/evidence.ts` — `ProviderEvidence` (provider kind · normalized issuer · subject · email attribute · verified/private-relay flags · assurance · optional display name) with total structural validation (`validateProviderEvidence`) · `providers/adapter.ts` — `AuthProviderAdapter` port returning typed `{ok,evidence} | {ok:false, invalidProviderEvidence|providerUnavailable}`; network I/O documented as strictly outside DB transactions · `providers/fake/fake-adapter.ts` — deterministic fake (mint token ↔ evidence, malformed-material minting, outage injection); the adapter ALL automated tests use · `providers/cognito/config.ts` — config types + env parser (returns undefined when no pool is configured — the universal B2-2 state; https-only issuer, ≥1 client id, derived JWKS URI) · `providers/cognito/cognito-adapter.ts` — JWKS validation via `jose@5` (RS256-only, issuer + audience + expiry + `token_use:'id'`, kid-rotation-aware injectable key source, remote JWKS default), claim normalization (Cognito `identities` → apple/google/email, string-or-boolean `email_verified`, private-relay suffix, `amr` → assurance), error normalization (JOSE token errors → invalidProviderEvidence; JWKS transport/non-JOSE → providerUnavailable); **`cognito:groups`/custom role claims read by nothing** · `mail/mail-sender.ts` — `MailSender` port + `CaptureMailSender` (captures, never delivers) + `mailLogLine` (template + recipient digest; the only sanctioned log form — no body/subject/address) · `persistence/identity-repository.ts` — ALL identity-table access (users, identities incl. FOR-UPDATE active-method listing and CAS ending, verified-email owner lookup, account+self-participant creation as one unit, challenge bookkeeping CAS) taking the caller's `Trx` · `services/account-status.ts` — `classifyAccountState` (user security state first, then customer-account business state; unknown user = deleted-shaped) + `readAccountState` · `services/first-login.ts` · `services/link-identity.ts` · `services/challenges.ts`.

**Service semantics (docs/26 §3, §9.1–9.3):** `firstLogin` — one transaction: resolve by issuer+subject → `identityResolved` (touches `last_login_at`; creates a missing customer account + self participant for a user first signing in as a customer, per §1.3) · else verified-email ownership precheck → create User + AuthIdentity + CustomerAccount + one `Me` self participant + audit + outbox atomically → `newCustomerCreated`. Concurrency: identical concurrent first logins converge by a single retry on the convergent unique constraints (issuer+subject, one-account-per-user, one-self-participant); cross-user verified-email races translate the 0003 exclusion violation to `verifiedEmailConflict`; **unexpected DB failures propagate as failures, never mislabeled as conflicts**. `linkIdentity(ctx, evidence)` — target user ONLY from the `AuthenticatedContext` abstraction (B2-4 supplies it after step-up): idempotent `identityAlreadyLinked`, permanent-subject `identityLinkedToAnotherUser` (active OR ended elsewhere), own-ended-row reactivation, same-user shared verified email allowed, cross-user `verifiedEmailConflict`, retry-once convergence under races. `unlinkIdentity` — §9.3 row-locked usable-method count (FOR UPDATE on all active identities serializes concurrent unlinks; a user can never lose the final method), version-CAS ending → `staleVersion`, cross-user rows not-found-shaped. Challenges — bookkeeping only (codes are the provider's): requested + audit, open-only attempt counting, CAS single-use completion; concurrent completion admits one winner. Account state: locked/deleted user → `accountLocked`/`accountDeleted`; suspended/anonymized account → `accountSuspended`/`accountDeleted` at login; login-method management gates on user security state only.

**Typed outcomes:** `identityResolved` · `newCustomerCreated` · `identityLinked` · `identityAlreadyLinked` · `identityLinkedToAnotherUser` · `verifiedEmailConflict` · `identityEnded` (ended identity presented as login; maps to §10 `accountLinkConflict` guidance at route time) · `identityUnlinked` · `lastLoginMethod` · `identityNotFound` · `staleVersion` · `accountLocked` · `accountDeleted` · `accountSuspended` · `invalidProviderEvidence` · `providerUnavailable` (adapter) · `challengeInvalid`/`recorded`/`completed`. No raw constraint names or provider errors cross the service boundary.

**Audit/outbox events (same transaction as the causal mutation, ids-only payloads):** audit `auth.user_created` / `auth.account_created` / `auth.identity_linked` / `auth.identity_unlinked` / `auth.challenge_requested` / `auth.challenge_completed`; outbox `user.created` (aggregate app_user) / `account.created` (aggregate customer_account) / `identity.linked` / `identity.unlinked` (aggregate app_user).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **163/163 across 16 suites** (77 new across 7 new suites) on real PostgreSQL, every DB suite migrating from zero through 0003 · first-login: single-transaction creation proven, audit+outbox atomicity, forced-failure rollback (participant-insert trigger) leaves zero rows/events, idempotent repeat, 4-way concurrent convergence to one user/account, issuer+subject-not-email matching, unverified duplicates independent, cross-user verified-email refusal incl. concurrent one-winner, locked/deleted/suspended typed, malformed evidence rejected without DB contact, missing-account backfill on first customer sign-in · linking: link/idempotent/other-user (active + ended)/reactivation/shared-verified-email/cross-user-conflict/concurrent one-winner subject race/concurrent idempotent convergence/locked/deleted/malformed · unlink: ended+events, last-method refusal, not-found shaping, staleVersion, concurrent unlinks cannot strand a user · challenges: audit'd request, attempt counting, CAS single-use, expiry, concurrent single winner · adapter contract suite over BOTH fake and Cognito (valid → normalized evidence; invalid/garbage → typed; outage → providerUnavailable) · Cognito specifics: forged key, wrong issuer, wrong audience, expired, `token_use` access rejected, missing sub, JWKS rotation (new kid validates after key-set refresh), groups/custom-claims ignored and absent from evidence, Apple federation + private relay, string `email_verified`, `amr` mfa, config parsing (undefined when unset · https-only · client-id required) · boundary guards: jose/AWS imports only inside `providers/cognito/`; no Cognito claim names in the core; services free of direct table access and of console/stdout logging; **full service flows executed AS `himma_app`** (role-GUC pool) proving grants sufficient (breadth denials remain covered by B2-1 suites); outbox payload values are UUIDs only; audit rows carry no emails and no digests · CaptureMailSender records full messages, delivers nothing; `mailLogLine` contains template + digest only · migrate-from-zero + `db:verify` green (3 migrations; no new migration in B2-2) · Kysely codegen re-run: zero drift · root untouched (no repository-level change outside `backend/`; root certification not re-run). New backend dependency: `jose@^5` (zero-dependency JWT/JWKS; v5 chosen because v6 is ESM-only and the backend is CJS).

**Recorded deviations/decisions (docs/26 reconciliation):** §9.1's `login_session` insert is deliberately absent — sessions are the B2-3 commit per §13 (owner-directed split; first-login returns the resolved principal for B2-3 to layer sessions onto) · the docs/26 §13 B2-2 stop-line's real-Cognito-adapter exercise against a live pool is NOT done — this task prohibited AWS access; JWKS mechanics are proven against locally generated keys, and the §14.E′ dev-pool smoke remains an explicit outstanding item · `identityEnded` extends the core vocabulary (route mapping to `accountLinkConflict` guidance recorded in code) · the mail port ships standalone; no flow sends mail yet (enumeration-safe flows are B2-4, per D2).

### B2-2 correction (2026-08-06 — owner conditional approval of `559ad2c`; this commit, awaiting owner review)

**Final display-name rule (binding, owner-directed):** a customer's display name is a validated provider-supplied name when present; otherwise the neutral placeholder `Customer` (the explicit profile-incomplete default pending W1 onboarding). **A person's name is never inferred from an email address** — the B2-2 email-local-part fallback is removed, and Apple private-relay addresses and ordinary addresses receive identical treatment. Blank/oversized provider names are dropped by evidence validation and fall to the neutral placeholder. The self participant remains `Me` (docs/24 §1.2, never email-derived). Identity matching, verified-email ownership, linking, and all other B2-2 behavior are unchanged — the only production edit is `defaultDisplayName` in [first-login.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/first-login.ts).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** 5 new focused tests (valid provider name retained · absent name → `Customer`, never the local part · private-relay local part never a name · blank and oversized names → `Customer` · repeat first login never rewrites the stored name) — the local-part cases observed failing before the fix · repeat/concurrent first-login suites unchanged and green · backend Jest **168/168 across 16 suites** · tsc clean · ESLint clean. No schema, migration, or dependency change.

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-3 (2026-08-06 — sessions and liveness, this commit, awaiting owner review)

Implemented per docs/26 §13 B2-3 (§4.4–4.7, §9.4–9.6, §9.8 revocation leg) on the approved B2-2 core. **No new migration** (the B2-1 `login_session` schema already carries every approved field). **No HTTP routes, no Fastify hooks** (docs/26's "liveness middleware" is delivered as the framework-neutral service B2-4's middleware will wrap — per the owner's B2-3 directive), no admin services, no MFA, no cloud resource, no region choice.

**Modules introduced:** `providers/access-token.ts` — the **separate access-token verification boundary**: `AccessTokenEvidence` (issuer · subject · `origin_jti` · optional `jti`/client id · scopes · assurance · expiry) with total structural validation; an ID token is never an API bearer token · `providers/fake/fake-access-token-verifier.ts` — deterministic fake used by all session tests · `providers/cognito/cognito-access-token-verifier.ts` — RS256-only JWKS verification (injectable kid-rotation-aware key source), trusted issuer, **`client_id` claim** (access tokens carry no `aud`), **`token_use='access'`** (ID tokens rejected by construction), exp/nbf with **bounded configurable clock tolerance** (config `clockToleranceSeconds` 0–300, default 30, parser-validated), normalized subject + `origin_jti` required, scopes/`amr` carried as transport metadata; groups/custom claims read by nothing · `providers/revocation.ts` — `ProviderSessionRevoker` port (`session`/`allSessions` scope, ephemeral client-supplied token material passed through but **never persisted or logged**) + typed post-commit `ProviderRevocationDelivery` for retry processing · `providers/fake/fake-provider-revoker.ts` (records presence of ephemeral material, never the material) · `persistence/session-repository.ts` — all `login_session` access (insert, by-origin_jti, FOR-UPDATE by id, live-by-user, touch, revoke-if-live, version-CAS revoke, revoke-all-live) · `services/sessions.ts` — `establishSession` + `listSessions` + `digestIp` (sha256; raw IPs never at rest) + the shared `resolveActivePrincipal` gate · `services/session-liveness.ts` — `checkSessionLiveness` returning the framework-neutral `AuthenticatedSessionPrincipal` (**no roles — provider claims never create Himma permissions**; read-only by default, `touch` opt-in) · `services/session-revocation.ts` — `logoutSession` (current device without version / selected device with CAS), `logoutAllSessions`, `forceRevokeUserSessions` (reasons account_locked/suspended/deleted/security, composable into future account-status flows), post-commit provider follow-up.

**Session rules (documented in code + proven):** a session exists only for verified evidence whose issuer+subject resolves to an active identity of an active user/account · **one provider session (origin_jti) maps to at most one Himma session EVER** — repeated/concurrent establishment converges on the live row (unique-index retry), and a revoked row permanently retires its origin_jti, so logout can never be undone by re-presenting an old token; a fresh provider login carries a fresh origin_jti · an origin_jti recorded for a different principal is refused as untrustworthy evidence · rows hold normalized metadata only — no token material anywhere (structural guards + no-refresh-token schema guard stay green) · liveness gates session → expiry → identity → user/account, denies revoked/expired/locked/suspended/deleted/ended, and once a revocation commits every subsequent check denies (in-flight checks that began earlier follow docs/26 §4.6 "refused on its next request") · local revocation is authoritative: reason + actor audit + version bump + outbox (`session.revoked_all` for set-wide) in one transaction; provider revocation runs strictly post-commit through the port, failure yields a typed status + `auth.provider_revocation_failed` audit and never reactivates the session · revoke-all is point-in-time (documented race rule): sessions committed at its snapshot are revoked; an establishment committing after survives as a new login · ownership enforced (another user's session is not-found-shaped) · concurrent logouts idempotent (`alreadyRevoked`); stale mutation refused (`staleVersion`).

**Typed outcomes added:** `sessionEstablished` · `sessionNotRegistered` · `sessionExpired` · `sessionRevoked` · `authenticated` (principal) · `invalidAccessToken` · `providerUnavailable` · `loggedOut` · `alreadyRevoked` · `sessionNotFound` · `loggedOutAll` · `sessionsRevoked` · `staleVersion` · plus the B2-2 account/identity states reused. Audit: `auth.session_established` · `auth.session_revoked` · `auth.provider_revocation_failed`. Outbox: `session.revoked_all` (aggregate app_user, ids-only/empty payload).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **223/223 across 20 suites** (55 new in 4 suites) on real PostgreSQL — access-token contract over both verifiers (valid accepted; **ID-token-as-bearer rejected**; missing `token_use`, wrong issuer, wrong client id, expired, not-yet-valid, forged signature, unknown kid, missing origin_jti all rejected; bounded skew tolerance honored and capped; groups/custom claims no effect and absent from evidence; scopes/mfa/`jti` carried; config fails closed without issuer/client ids and rejects out-of-range tolerance) · establishment (normalized metadata + digested IP only, audit event, idempotent + 4-way concurrent convergence to one live session, unknown/ended identity + locked/suspended gates, foreign-origin_jti refusal, forced-failure rollback leaves zero rows/events) · liveness (role-free principal, not-registered/revoked/expired distinctions, all account states, read-only by default, revocation-commit denial incl. concurrent storm) · revocation (reason/actor/version audit, concurrent idempotency with exactly one audit row, ownership, CAS staleVersion, revoke-all scoped to the intended user with one outbox event, forced revocation blocks all sessions, **provider called strictly after commit** — observed committed state at call time — with ephemeral material passed through and never retained, provider failure/throw normalized to typed status with local denial intact, point-in-time race rule sequential + concurrent) · secrecy sweeps (no token-like material in rows, audit, or outbox) · migrate-from-zero + `db:verify` green (3 migrations, none new) · codegen zero drift · B2-1 + B2-2 suites untouched and green · root untouched. **The boundary guards caught and forced two fixes during implementation** (a service-level direct table read moved into the repository; a provider claim-name literal removed from the core port) — the scans are load-bearing.

**Recorded deviations/decisions:** docs/26 §13's "liveness middleware" delivered as the framework-neutral `checkSessionLiveness` service per the owner's B2-3 directive — B2-4's Fastify middleware wraps it · the real-pool smoke (§14.E′ non-production dev pool) remains outstanding; local-key JWKS tests are NOT claimed as live Cognito validation · a Cognito `ProviderSessionRevoker` implementation is deliberately NOT built (one-device revocation needs client-held refresh-token material and pool endpoints; the port + orchestration + typed delivery status are in place, the concrete revoker lands with the dev-pool prerequisite) · `establishSession` treats a revoked origin_jti as permanently retired (`sessionRevoked`) — the schema-level post-revocation key reuse proven in B2-1 remains possible for elevated tooling but the service path refuses resurrection.

### B2-3 identity-spine consistency review (2026-08-06 — owner conditional approval of `345659e`; proof, this commit, awaiting owner review)

**Finding: the binding rule is already structurally guaranteed — no production change was required; the deliverable is a proof suite.** Inspection record: (1) the B2-2 ID-token adapter verifies the token `iss` against the configured pool issuer and emits `evidence.issuer = config.issuer`, `evidence.subject = payload.sub` — in a Cognito user pool, `sub` is the pool-assigned user identifier, identical in ID and access tokens; the Apple/Google external subject exists only inside the `identities` claim, which the adapter reads solely to derive the `provider` provenance kind (apple/google/email), never as a key. (2) First login therefore persists `auth_identity` keyed by **(pool issuer, Cognito sub)** — the canonical Cognito identity; `provider` is metadata. (3) The B2-3 access-token verifier emits the same `(config.issuer, payload.sub)` pair, so establishment and liveness resolve through the identical key by construction — external identities are not merely "not required" for bearer authentication, they are unreachable by it (the verifier can only ever stamp the configured pool issuer). (4) `login_session`'s composite FK `(user_id, provider_issuer, provider_subject) → auth_identity(user_id, issuer, subject)` ties every session to that canonical row at the schema level. (5) Apple/Google/email flows all reduce to the same key; email and groups remain non-authoritative (B2-2/B2-3 guards).

**Proof suite [identity-spine-consistency.test.ts](backend/test/identity-spine-consistency.test.ts)** (9 tests; real Cognito adapters over local JWKS + real services on real PostgreSQL): Apple-federated ID token (external `identities.userId` present) persists pool issuer + Cognito sub and the external subject appears nowhere as a key · ID-token and access-token evidence carry the identical (issuer, subject) for one user · Apple-federated, Google-federated, and email/password logins each followed by access-token establishment resolve one user, with the session row FK-tied to the canonical identity and liveness authenticating the same principal · a deliberately linked external Apple-issuer identity coexists as an additional row without replacing or conflicting with the canonical identity, and post-link access tokens still resolve the canonical row · two Cognito users sharing an unverified email stay separate end-to-end · a second Cognito user claiming an owned verified email gets `verifiedEmailConflict` with no merge · concurrent first logins and session establishments converge on one user and one live session.

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend Jest **232/232 across 21 suites** (9 new) · tsc clean · ESLint clean · no schema change (schema verification/codegen unchanged per the owner directive) · no production code modified.

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-4 (2026-08-06 — auth routes and principal context, this commit, awaiting owner review)

Implemented per docs/26 §13 B2-4 (§10 customer routes under A1.1, §6 policy layers, §11.2/11.8–11.10) on the approved B2-2/B2-3 core. **No admin routes/services, no MFA, no customer login UI, no cloud resource, no production email, no region choice.**

**Route inventory (snapshot-guarded — [http-policy-pipeline.test.ts.snap](backend/test/__snapshots__/http-policy-pipeline.test.ts.snap); a future unprotected route breaks the suite):** `POST /auth/session` (unauthenticatedAuthFlow) · `POST /auth/password/reset-request` (unauthenticatedAuthFlow) · `GET /me`, `GET /auth/sessions`, `POST /auth/logout`, `POST /auth/logout-all` (authenticatedCustomer) · `DELETE /auth/sessions/:sessionId`, `POST /auth/identities/link`, `DELETE /auth/identities/:identityId` (stepUpRequired) · `GET /internal/health` (explicitly public). All bodies/params/responses TypeBox-validated; auth routes carry a 16 KB body limit; responses are nosniff and non-public routes no-store.

**Modules:** [policies.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/policies.ts) — four policy categories (`public` · `unauthenticatedAuthFlow` · `authenticatedCustomer` · `stepUpRequired`; role policies are a later-slice registry extension), **structural deny-by-default**: an undeclared route throws AT REGISTRATION (proven — the app cannot even construct), and the inventory is decorated for the snapshot test · [rate-limiter.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/rate-limiter.ts) — store abstraction + deterministic in-memory impl; **`createRateLimiterStore('production')` FAILS CLOSED** (no approved distributed store exists; a per-instance limiter is never shipped as production-safe); keys are sha256 digests (email/IP/user), never raw values; defaults (docs/26 §14.C): session 10/min·IP, reset 5/min·email+IP, link 10/min·user, invalid-bearer 10/min·IP · [auth-plugin.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/auth-plugin.ts) — bearer pipeline: strict header shape/length → ACCESS-token verifier (ID tokens rejected by token-usage semantics; provider I/O outside transactions) → B2-3 `checkSessionLiveness` → minimum principal (`RequestPrincipal` = B2-3 session principal + issuer/subject + `stepUpAt` + typed EMPTY `adminRoles`/`orgScope` slots — nothing fabricated, no provider claim ever creates a permission); step-up = provider `auth_time` within the configurable max-age (default 300 s; new optional `authTime` on AccessTokenEvidence, mapped from `auth_time`/`iat`); repeated invalid bearers rate-limited per IP digest · [http-outcomes.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/http-outcomes.ts) — one stable `{code,message}` envelope; documented collapses: locked/suspended/deleted → `accountSuspended` (§3.8 single message), all bearer session denials → `sessionExpired`, login-flow failures → `invalidCredentials`; nothing leaks constraints/JWKS/stacks/existence · [identity-routes.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/identity-routes.ts) + services [profile.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/profile.ts) (approved `/me` snapshot: user id · account id/displayName/contactEmail · self participant · empty roles; no security metadata, no child legal fields) and [password-reset.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/password-reset.ts) (enumeration-safe: identical outward result; bookkeeping + captured mail only when an identity exists; mail carries NO code — codes are the provider's) · [build-app.ts](backend/src/app/build-app.ts) wires it all behind injected deps.

**`POST /auth/session` orchestration (documented failure boundary):** access token verified → optional ID token verified → **token-pair binding: both must carry the same issuer + subject, else 401 with nothing created** → B2-2 `firstLogin` (own transaction) → B2-3 `establishSession` (own transaction) — deliberately NOT one spanning transaction (the §13 commit split separates them); a canonical user may exist when establishment fails (proven via forced trigger → 500 internalError, user exists), and a retry converges with zero duplicate users/accounts/sessions (both services are idempotent/convergent). Unknown identities on the login path collapse into `invalidCredentials` (§5.5), while unlink keeps not-found shaping.

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint clean · backend Jest **265/265 across 24 suites** (33 new in 3 HTTP suites) on real PostgreSQL — inventory snapshot + per-route policy assertion · undeclared route fails at registration · health public · missing/malformed/oversized bearer headers 401 without echo · fake-ID-token-as-bearer rejected · valid token + live session authenticates · not-registered/revoked → `sessionExpired`; locked/suspended/deleted → `accountSuspended` · provider outage → 503 · scopes/claims yield empty roles · 10-failure bearer limit → deterministic 429 + retry-after · stale `auth_time` → 403 `stepUpRequired` on step-up routes · production rate-store fail-closed · session route: establish, pair-mismatch rejection with zero rows, idempotent re-present, token-only for known identity, unknown → `invalidCredentials`, partial-failure 500 then convergent retry, per-IP 429, 422 without echoing values · `/me` exact approved keys (no `locked|ip_digest|origin_jti|version|status` text) · inventory scoped + `current` flag · selected logout: foreign 404, stale 409, ok 200, step-up enforced · logout idempotent + local denial after provider-revocation failure · logout-all revokes only the caller's sessions (count 2, bystander intact), repeat safely denied 401 · link: linked/alreadyLinked/409 for another user's subject · unlink: CAS + `lastLoginMethod` 409 · reset-request: byte-identical bodies/status/header-sets for existing vs unknown email, both ≥ the 60 ms test floor, captured mail only for the existing identity with no code-like content, schema 422, 429 after 5 per email digest · secrecy sweeps: no token/code material in audit, outbox, or any error payload · migrate-from-zero + `db:verify` green (3 migrations, none new) · codegen zero drift · B2-1/2/3 suites untouched and green · root untouched.

**Recorded deviations/decisions (docs/26 reconciliation):** §10's `login`/`oidc/apple`/`oidc/google` collapse into `POST /auth/session` under A1.1 (all providers converge on Cognito token presentation; nonce handling is provider-side) · `register`/`verify-email`/`login(email)` password flows need adapter CREDENTIAL mediation against a real pool and are deferred to the §14.E′ dev-pool prerequisite (recorded, not silently dropped) · `POST /auth/refresh` has no Himma route — rotation is Cognito-client-side (§9.4); Himma's leg is per-request liveness · `POST /auth/step-up` as a route is deferred with MFA (B2-6); step-up ENFORCEMENT ships now via provider `auth_time` recency (§3.10/§14.C) · reset-request is the one §10 recovery route shipped (challenge bookkeeping + notice; provider-side code dispatch joins with the dev pool) · ephemeral client-held refresh material passes through logout bodies to the provider-revocation port only — never persisted, logged, audited, or echoed.

**Still pending production dependencies:** §14.E′ non-production dev pool (real-pool smoke; Cognito revoker implementation; credential-mediation routes) · approved distributed rate-limit store (production limiter startup is refused until then) · production email delivery (D2) · production region/DR (docs/23 §18.3) · production Apple/Google client ids (docs/24 §14.B13).

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-5 (2026-08-06 — admin roles, dual control, bootstrap CLI, this commit, awaiting owner review)

Implemented per docs/26 §13 B2-5 (§7, §9.7, §9.9, §10 admin routes, §11.6) on the approved B2-1..B2-4 stack. **No provider staff/organization roles, no MFA implementation, no cloud resource.**

**Services ([admin-roles.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/admin-roles.ts) over [admin-role-repository.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/persistence/admin-role-repository.ts)):** `resolveAdminRoles` — **direct database-backed resolution on every request** (documented rule: no caching, no role material in tokens; grants/revocations/expiry take effect on the next request); only `active` AND unexpired assignments qualify — requested/denied/revoked/time-expired are ignored (proven) · `requestRoleAssignment` — only an active access_admin may request; finance-capable roles (`finance`, `access_admin`) enter `requested`; other roles activate immediately on the single access-admin request (§7.2) · `approveRoleAssignment` (distinct-approver pre-typed `selfApprovalForbidden`, CHECK-backed) · `denyRoleAssignment` · `revokeRoleAssignment` · `processExpiredAssignments` sweep (resolution already ignores overdue rows) · `listRoleAssignments`/`getRoleAssignment` (readers: access_admin + auditor). All mutations are single transactions with audit (`auth.admin_role_requested/approved/denied/revoked/expired`, `auth.bootstrap_completed`) + outbox (`admin_role.requested/approved/denied/revoked`, ids-only payloads); the B2-1/0003 constraints and triggers remain the final authority — the service pre-checks for typed outcomes and TRANSLATES database refusals (trigger-message classification stays internal; nothing DB-shaped reaches HTTP/CLI). Platform-engineer separation stays an infrastructure-IAM + grant-time rule, deliberately NOT misrepresented as database-enforced. Typed outcomes: `roleRequested/roleActivated/roleDenied/roleRevoked/requesterNotQualified/approverNotQualified/selfApprovalForbidden/targetNotFound/assignmentNotFound/assignmentAlreadyFinalized/roleConflict/staleVersion/forbidden`.

**Admin routes ([admin-routes.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/admin-routes.ts), snapshot-updated intentionally):** new explicit `admin` policy category — live session + **MFA assurance** (`mfaRequired` typed refusal otherwise) + ≥1 active Himma database role (`forbidden` otherwise); Cognito groups/claims still create nothing. `GET /admin/role-assignments` · `GET /admin/role-assignments/:id` · `POST /admin/role-requests` · `POST /admin/role-requests/:id/approve` · `POST /admin/role-requests/:id/deny` · `DELETE /admin/role-assignments/:id` — TypeBox schemas, stable envelopes (`dualControlViolation` 409 for self-approval, `roleConflict`/`assignmentAlreadyFinalized`/`staleVersion` 409, `forbidden`/`mfaRequired` 403, `notFound` 404). **Production fail-closed boundary (until B2-6): admin routes are NOT registered in production builds (absent → 404), and explicitly forcing `enableAdminRoutes` in production THROWS at startup** — admin business access cannot become production-enabled through B2-5; B2-6's admin MFA enforcement lifts the gate.

**Bootstrap ([bootstrap.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/admin/bootstrap.ts) + CLI `npm run bootstrap:admins`):** CLI-only (no HTTP path) — requires explicit production env, the reviewed manifest `{users:[a,b], secretDigest}`, the exact confirmation phrase, and the secret via `HIMMA_BOOTSTRAP_SECRET`/interactive input (never argv); manifest/users (exactly two distinct, active, verified-identity) validated BEFORE any transaction; sha256 digest binds the secret to the reviewed manifest (timing-safe compare); one transaction under an advisory lock: exactly two cross-witnessed access_admin assignments + audit + outbox + the immutable seal (manifest digest + operator ref) — the implementation has NO role parameter, so no other role is creatable; concurrent attempts admit exactly one success; sealed forever after; forced failure rolls back everything; himma_app cannot touch the seal (B2-1 guarantee, still green). Secret lifecycle documented in the module header (generate ≥32 chars into the store at review, digest into the manifest, inject once, destroy); never logged/persisted — proven by sweep.

**Seeding ([seed.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/admin/seed.ts) + CLI `npm run db:seed-admins`):** entirely separate mechanism — refuses production, refuses sealed/foreign-state databases, never writes a seal, never calls the bootstrap implementation; deterministic identities (fixed seed subjects, `.invalid` emails) and safely repeatable (converges). Because 0003 only admits a first cross-pair inside the seal transaction, seeding suspends exactly the one finance-approver constraint trigger inside its own elevated transaction and restores it — proven not to weaken the database (a sealless conspiracy pair is still refused after seeding).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-06):** backend tsc clean · ESLint clean · backend Jest **291/291 across 27 suites** (26 new: 9 service, 10 bootstrap/seeding, 7 route) on real PostgreSQL — resolution ignores every non-qualifying state · immediate vs dual-control activation · self-approval refusal at service and CHECK level · unqualified requester/approver refusals · D4 conflicts via service AND trigger · concurrent double-approval single-activation · concurrent approval-vs-revocation safe in every interleaving · stale CAS · expiry pre-sweep and sweep · customer 403 on admin routes, non-MFA admin `mfaRequired`, no-bearer 401 · full route lifecycle incl. conflict/stale/deny/inspect (no emails in responses) · production build: admin routes absent + forced enablement throws · full bootstrap rehearsal on fresh scratch DBs: env/manifest/secret/phrase gates create nothing, forced seal failure rolls back all, concurrency single-winner, exactly two access_admins with audit+outbox+seal, permanently sealed, structurally-single-role, secret-free sweeps · seeding: production refusal, seal untouched, deterministic repeatability, post-seed constraint integrity · migrate-from-zero + `db:verify` green (3 migrations, none new) · codegen zero drift · B2-1..B2-4 suites green (policy snapshot updated intentionally with the six admin routes) · root untouched.

**Recorded deviations/decisions:** the bootstrap manifest binds its secret by sha256 digest (the mechanism docs/26 D3 left to implementation; no secret-store vendor selected) · ~~bootstrap outbox uses the approved `admin_role.approved` events (×2) with `auth.bootstrap_completed` as audit — no new outbox event type invented~~ *(superseded by the event-integrity correction below: a dedicated `admin.bootstrap.completed` outbox event now exists alongside them)* · ~~expiry finalization is audit-only (no approved outbox event)~~ *(superseded below: the sweep now emits `admin_role.expired` audit + outbox atomically)* · seeding's single-trigger suspension is the recorded consequence of the 0003 conspiracy protection.

### B2-5 event-integrity correction (2026-08-07 — owner conditional approval of `5c1c98f`; correction `99575a4`, **fully approved with B2-5 the same day**)

Narrowly scoped per the owner's conditional approval; all existing B2-5 behavior preserved (every prior event, gate, and typed outcome unchanged — the correction only ADDS the two missing event emissions).

**1 · Bootstrap completion event ([bootstrap.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/admin/bootstrap.ts)):** the production bootstrap now appends a dedicated transactional-outbox event `admin.bootstrap.completed` (aggregate `bootstrap_seal` / manifest digest) in the SAME transaction as the two Access Administrator assignments, their role-approval events, the immutable seal, and the existing `auth.bootstrap_completed` audit event. Payload is safe identifiers only: `manifestDigest` (the singleton seal's canonical identifier — the seal table has no surrogate id by design), the two `assignmentIds`, the two `userIds`, `sealedAt` (read back from the inserted seal row), and `executedBy` (the reviewed ticket/operator ref) — no secret, no manifest contents, no emails, no tokens. The existing advisory-lock single-winner path means concurrent attempts produce exactly one completion event; a failed bootstrap rolls the completion event back with everything else.

**2 · Expiry events ([admin-roles.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/admin-roles.ts) + [admin-role-repository.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/persistence/admin-role-repository.ts)):** the expiry sweep's canonical `active → expired` transition now emits the existing `auth.admin_role_expired` audit event AND a new `admin_role.expired` outbox event (aggregate `app_user`/target, payload `{assignmentId}` only) in the same transaction as the state change; `expireDueAssignments` returns `{id, user_id}` pairs to carry the aggregate id. Merely reading an overdue assignment emits nothing — only the sweep's transition does. Already-expired rows are untouched (no duplicates); concurrent sweeps converge via row locks (each assignment transitions and emits exactly once); principal resolution continues denying time-expired assignments immediately, before any sweep.

**Tests ([admin-event-integrity.test.ts](backend/test/admin-event-integrity.test.ts), 9 new, real PostgreSQL, TDD red→green):** successful bootstrap writes exactly one completion audit + one completion outbox event · payload carries exactly the safe identifiers (verified field-by-field against the seal and assignment rows) · no secret and no email address in any event payload (bootstrap + expiry) · forced failure at the completion-event write (trigger on `outbox_event`) rolls back assignments, seal, and every bootstrap event atomically · concurrent bootstrap attempts still produce exactly one completion event · forced failure at the expiry outbox write rolls the state transition back (atomicity), then the clean sweep transitions + emits both events · repeated sweeps are idempotent (no duplicate events) · two concurrent sweeps over three due assignments emit exactly one transition/event set each · pre-sweep resolution denial re-proven.

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-07):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **300/300 across 28 suites** (9 new) on real PostgreSQL · migrate-from-zero on a fresh scratch database (3 migrations, none new) · `db:verify` passed · codegen zero drift (12 tables, generated file byte-identical) · no frontend or native surface touched (no recertification required).

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-6A (2026-08-07 — MFA, recovery-code, and step-up schema foundation; **FULLY APPROVED at `0756456` 2026-08-07**)

Owner-directed sub-slice of B2-6: **schema only** (migration `0004_mfa_and_step_up_foundation.sql`, regenerated Kysely types, focused real-PostgreSQL tests). No Cognito calls, no application services, no HTTP routes, no admin-login activation, no cloud resource, no frontend. Cognito remains authoritative for TOTP secrets and provider MFA configuration (Amendment A1.1 supersedes the §5.8 pre-amendment secret-storage wording — read as an applied amendment, not a contradiction); Himma stores only enforcement/audit/recovery/assurance bookkeeping.

**Tables (all Slice-1 conventions: UUIDv7 ids, UTC timestamptz, created/updated/version + shared triggers, CHECK-backed states, FK ownership):**
- **`mfa_method`** — Cognito-managed TOTP enrollment mirror (docs/26 §8.8). `kind` CHECK `'totp'` only; `provider_managed` CHECK-forced true (a Himma-managed method would imply secret storage — structurally impossible). Lifecycle `pending → active → disabled | superseded`: pending requires `enrollment_expires_at` and no confirmation; active requires `confirmed_at`; terminal states require `ended_at` and are frozen by trigger. Partial uniques: one active AND one pending method per user. Trigger `enforce_mfa_method_transition` also refuses activation past the enrollment window and makes `confirmed_at` write-once, settable only by the pending→active transition (so B2-6B's activation is a single atomic UPDATE after Cognito verification succeeds).
- **`app_user.mfa_enrolled` mirror** — maintained BOTH ways by trigger: `maintain_mfa_enrolled_mirror` (AFTER on `mfa_method`) recomputes it from active methods; `enforce_mfa_enrolled_consistency` (BEFORE on `app_user`) refuses any direct write disagreeing with the active-method truth — the flag alone can never fabricate enrollment, and it is bookkeeping, never recent-MFA assurance (runtime concern, B2-6B/C).
- **`mfa_recovery_code_batch`** — one identifiable issuance: `code_count` (>0; count is §14.C configuration), `digest_scheme` (`hmac_sha256`), `pepper_version` (≥1; the pepper value lives in the secret store, never PostgreSQL), `issued_at`/`ended_at`, states `active → superseded | invalidated` (terminal frozen; metadata immutable). Partial unique: one active batch per user. `UNIQUE (id, user_id)` is the composite-FK target so codes can never cross users. Cascade trigger `cascade_recovery_batch_invalidation`: when a batch leaves active, every remaining unconsumed code is invalidated in the same transaction — service omission cannot leave stale usable codes.
- **`mfa_recovery_code`** — one versioned digest per single-use code: globally unique `code_hash` (docs/26 uq), `consumed_at`/`invalidated_at` both write-once (trigger) and mutually exclusive (CHECK) — consumption is CAS, concurrency admits exactly one winner, and no consumed/invalidated code can ever be restored or its digest rewritten. Raw codes exist only transiently in the future issuing service.
- **`mfa_challenge`** — challenge bookkeeping (§8.6 pattern extended for MFA; the 0002 `auth_challenge` stays untouched — it is identity-bound with email/reset kinds, while MFA challenges are user/session-bound, recorded as a DDL-review decision). `purpose` `login | step_up` (step_up requires a session binding; login precedes any Himma session on the `mfaRequired` continuation), composite FK `(login_session_id, user_id)` so a challenge can never bind another user's session, `expires_at` NOT NULL, monotonic `attempt_count`, states `pending → passed | failed | expired | invalidated` (terminal frozen; expired can never pass; `passed_at` ⇔ passed). No provider challenge material — Cognito's challenge session travels client↔adapter only.
- **`step_up_grant`** — §3.10 recent-reauthentication bookkeeping backing `PrincipalContext.stepUpAt`: bound by composite FK to the owning user + login session, `method` `totp | recovery_code | password | oidc`, bounded window CHECK (`expires_at > granted_at`; max-age is §14.C configuration), immutable except one-time `invalidated_at`. A grant row alone proves nothing: runtime assurance additionally requires the verified B2-6B flow, a live session, and an unexpired uninvalidated grant (documented on the table).
- **`login_session`** gains `UNIQUE (id, user_id)` (`uq_login_session_id_user`) as the composite-FK target.

**Prohibited-material posture:** no column anywhere can hold TOTP secrets, QR contents, raw recovery codes, Cognito session tokens, access/refresh tokens, challenge secrets, or encryption/hash keys — proven structurally (exact column-set locks on `mfa_method`/`mfa_recovery_code` + expanded prohibited-name scan over every public column; the B2-1 secret-column guard also still covers all new tables). Grants: `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` (never DELETE) on all five tables to `himma_app`; retention classes documented per table (challenges/grants short-lived operational records for the elevated sweep; methods/batches follow the account lifecycle, pseudonymization-ready).

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-07):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **324/324 across 29 suites** (24 new in [mfa-schema.test.ts](backend/test/mfa-schema.test.ts), TDD red→green: lifecycle/invalid-combination refusals · one-active-and-one-pending method per user · expired-enrollment activation refused · terminal immutability · mirror follows only active methods, direct writes refused at insert and update · one active batch per user · duplicate digests and cross-user codes refused · regeneration invalidates remaining-not-consumed codes · CAS single consumption · concurrent consumption single winner · no restore/rewrite of consumed/invalidated codes or digests · step-up challenge session binding + foreign-session refusal · expired/terminal challenges cannot pass/reuse · monotonic attempts · grant user+session binding, bounded window, write-once invalidation · structural secret-absence · himma_app grant matrix) · migrate-from-zero (4 migrations) · `db:verify` passed (0004 objects registered in SCHEMA_CHECKS) · **rollback verified**: `db:down` (with its confirmation gate) cleanly reverted 0004, re-apply restored, verify green — the migrations suite's down-and-restore test now exercises 0004 automatically · codegen: 5 new interfaces, byte-identical on re-run (zero drift) · all B2-1..B2-5 identity/session/HTTP/admin-role/bootstrap suites green unchanged.

**Recorded deviations/decisions (within §8's "proposed; final DDL reviewed" latitude):** lifecycle columns (`state`, `enrollment_expires_at`, `ended_at`) extend §8.8's minimal method shape to model pending-expiry and disabled/superseded as the owner's B2-6A directive requires · recovery codes gain the batch table (docs/26 names only the code rows; batches realize §5.9 regeneration-invalidates-old-set as a database guarantee) · `mfa_challenge` is a new table rather than widening 0002 `auth_challenge` (different binding: user/session vs identity; applied migrations are immutable) · `step_up_grant` persists §3.10/PrincipalContext `stepUpAt` (per-request DB resolution needs persistent backing; docs/26 left the mechanism to implementation) · no production pepper value chosen — `pepper_version` metadata only.

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-6B (2026-08-07 — Cognito MFA mediation, recovery-code and step-up services, this commit, awaiting owner review)

Owner-directed sub-slice: provider-independent MFA application services over the approved B2-6A schema. **No HTTP routes, no production admin activation, no frontend, no Cognito pool or cloud resource, no region/email decisions.** Authority boundary held throughout: Cognito owns TOTP secret generation, provider-side enrollment, and challenge verification; Himma PostgreSQL owns mirrored enrollment state, recovery batches/consumption, challenge bookkeeping, step-up grants, audit, outbox, and every business authorization decision.

**MFA provider port ([mfa.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/mfa.ts)):** ~~`beginTotpEnrollment` / `verifyTotpEnrollment` / `verifyTotpChallenge`~~ *(superseded by the TOTP-semantics correction below: step-up now uses the distinct `beginTotpStepUpChallenge`/`respondToTotpStepUpChallenge` challenge-flow operations)*, normalized to `enrollmentStarted · verificationSucceeded · invalidCode · challengeExpired · providerUnavailable · invalidProviderState`. Called strictly OUTSIDE PostgreSQL transactions. [FakeMfaProvider](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/fake/fake-mfa-provider.ts) is the deterministic test double; [CognitoMfaAdapter](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/cognito/cognito-mfa-adapter.ts) mediates AssociateSoftwareToken/VerifySoftwareToken behind an INJECTED `CognitoMfaClient` interface (no AWS SDK dependency added; the SDK-backed client is instantiated only by the future §14.E′ real-pool smoke task) and collapses every provider error into single-field results by error NAME — no AWS exception object, message, or challenge state escapes. Provider-side MFA disable is NOT implemented: docs/26 assigns no standalone disable operation to this sub-slice (removal is a step-up-gated later concern; support-assisted reset is W3 tooling) — replacement happens only via re-enrollment supersession.

**TOTP enrollment ([mfa-enrollment.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/mfa-enrollment.ts)):** `beginTotpEnrollment` — eligibility (active user) → provider call outside any transaction → one transaction superseding any stale pending enrollment and inserting the pending `mfa_method` with the configured expiring window → audit `auth.mfa_enrollment_started`; the shared secret is returned ONLY as the ephemeral service result (for the future B2-6C route/QR) and never persisted/logged/audited. `completeTotpEnrollment` — pending validation (one normalized `enrollmentInvalid` for absent/foreign/non-pending/expired) → provider verification outside the transaction → only on success one activation transaction: supersede the previous active method (audit + outbox `mfa.method_superseded`), CAS-activate the pending method (expiry re-checked in-statement; B2-6A trigger beneath; any late failure aborts the WHOLE transaction), the DATABASE maintains `app_user.mfa_enrolled`, audit `auth.mfa_enrolled` + outbox `mfa.enrolled`. Provider failure leaves no falsely active method (failure audit `auth.mfa_enrollment_failed`); expired pending enrollments cannot activate even with stale valid provider material. Enrollment bookkeeping is the pending `mfa_method` row itself (B2-6A defines challenge purposes `login|step_up` only — recorded reading, not a contradiction).

**Recovery codes ([mfa-recovery-codes.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/mfa-recovery-codes.ts)):** generation uses `crypto.randomInt` (CSPRNG, unbiased) over a 30-symbol unambiguous alphabet (no 0/1/i/l/o/u), 28 symbols grouped `xxxx-…-xxxx` ≈ **137 bits ≥ 128**; entry is lenient (case/separators stripped, entropy untouched). The configured count (default 10 per §5.9; §14.C-configurable) is generated in memory, returned EXACTLY once; PostgreSQL receives only **HMAC-SHA-256 digests under the batch's pepper version**; regeneration supersedes the previous active batch in the same transaction (B2-6A cascade invalidates its remaining codes) with audit `auth.recovery_codes_generated` + outbox `mfa.recovery_codes_generated`. No operation anywhere can return an original code again. `consumeRecoveryCode` — digest computed with the BATCH's pepper version, CAS consumption (exactly one concurrent winner), wrong/consumed/unknown/superseded all collapse into ONE `recoveryCodeRejected` outcome (audited), and with a live session the approved step-up grant (`method 'recovery_code'`) is created in the SAME transaction with `auth.step_up_completed` + `mfa.step_up_completed`; a dead session refuses without burning the code.

**Pepper configuration ([mfa-config.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/mfa-config.ts)):** `HIMMA_MFA_RECOVERY_PEPPER` (+ `_VERSION`) through the docs/23 §10.3 secret/config boundary only; held in memory for HMAC, never persisted/logged. **Production fails closed**: missing, short (<32), or dev-valued peppers throw at config parse; development/test default to the deterministic `DEV_TEST_PEPPER` constant (clearly non-production; no production secret exists anywhere in the repo). All §14.C values configurable with safe defaults: recovery-code count 10 · enrollment window 900 s · challenge TTL 300 s · challenge attempt cap 5 · step-up max-age 900 s. A batch whose pepper version is not configured throws loudly (never a silent "invalid code").

**TOTP step-up ([mfa-step-up.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/mfa-step-up.ts)):** `beginStepUpChallenge` (live owned session + active method → pending session-bound B2-6A challenge with TTL, audited) · `completeStepUpWithTotp` — bookkeeping pre-check (binding/pending/expiry/attempt-cap, finalizing expired→`expired` and capped→`failed` with the normalized **`tooManyAttempts`** outcome) → provider verification outside any transaction → on failure monotonic attempt bump + `auth.mfa_challenge_failed` (cap finalizes) → on success ONE transaction: session re-checked live, challenge CAS `pending→passed` (single-use; **concurrent replay admits exactly one winner**), one `step_up_grant` bound to the canonical user + live session with `granted_at`/`expires_at` from one clock (exact configured window), audit `auth.mfa_challenge_passed`/`auth.step_up_completed` + outbox `mfa.step_up_completed`. `resolveStepUpAssurance` (for B2-6C principal resolution) returns assurance ONLY for a live session with an unexpired uninvalidated grant — a revoked/expired session makes every grant useless; provider verification alone never grants business permissions.

**Audit/outbox vocabulary added:** audit `auth.mfa_enrollment_started/mfa_enrolled/mfa_enrollment_failed/mfa_method_superseded/recovery_codes_generated/recovery_code_used/recovery_code_rejected/mfa_challenge_requested/mfa_challenge_failed/mfa_challenge_expired/mfa_challenge_passed/step_up_completed` · outbox `mfa.enrolled` (docs/26 §8.9) plus `mfa.method_superseded`, `mfa.recovery_codes_generated`, `mfa.recovery_code_used`, `mfa.step_up_completed`. Payloads are opaque ids + safe metadata only — no TOTP seed, OTP, raw code, digest, pepper, provider challenge/session string, or token (proven by whole-database sweeps).

**Secret-safety architecture:** [mfa-repository.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/persistence/mfa-repository.ts) input types structurally cannot accept secrets (ids, timestamps, and precomputed digests only; `insertRecoveryCodeDigests` is digest-named and digest-typed); services write no logs (B2-2 boundary guard covers the services directory); the adapter's normalized results are single-field objects (proven: provider errors carrying secret-bearing messages leave nothing behind); a whole-database sweep helper ([db-sweep.ts](backend/test/helpers/db-sweep.ts)) proves TOTP secrets, OTPs, raw/normalized codes, provider tokens, digests-in-events, and the pepper appear in NO row of ANY public table after full flows.

### B2-6B Cognito TOTP-semantics correction (2026-08-07 — owner conditional approval of `25dd742`; correction `49da921`, **fully approved with B2-6B the same day**)

Owner-directed review finding, confirmed: `25dd742`'s `verifyTotpChallenge` reused **VerifySoftwareToken — the Cognito ENROLLMENT/registration confirmation API — as a generic step-up verifier** against an access token. Cognito has no supported operation that verifies a bare TOTP code against an access token; an already-enrolled user's ordinary TOTP verification is the **SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA challenge flow**: a fresh reauthentication yields `ChallengeName: SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA` + a provider `Session`, answered via **RespondToAuthChallenge**. Corrected honestly, no invented capability:

- **Port** ([mfa.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/mfa.ts)): `verifyTotpChallenge` REMOVED. Enrollment keeps `beginTotpEnrollment`/`verifyTotpEnrollment` (Associate/VerifySoftwareToken only). Step-up gains two distinct operations: `beginTotpStepUpChallenge(providerUserRef) → challengeIssued{TotpChallengeSession}` and `respondToTotpStepUpChallenge(providerUserRef, challenge, code)`. `TotpChallengeSession` is documented ephemeral SECRET material — never persisted, logged, audited, or eventable; it travels only through the bounded begin→complete flow (service result → client → completion input).
- **Cognito adapter** ([cognito-mfa-adapter.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/cognito/cognito-mfa-adapter.ts)): injected `CognitoMfaClient` gains `initiateSoftwareTokenMfaChallenge` (the reauthentication call yielding the SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA challenge — WHICH Cognito auth flow produces it is pool configuration, deferred to §14.E′) and `respondToSoftwareTokenMfaChallenge` (RespondToAuthChallenge). The adapter refuses any challenge start that is not `SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA`-with-session; in the respond context `NotAuthorizedException` (invalid/expired/replayed Session) maps to `challengeExpired` — mapping flagged for confirmation in the §14.E′ real-pool smoke. No cloud provisioning; no AWS SDK added.
- **Service** ([mfa-step-up.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/services/mfa-step-up.ts)): `beginStepUpChallenge` now models the honest flow — eligibility → **fresh provider reauthentication challenge outside any transaction** (failure creates no Himma bookkeeping) → non-secret Himma `mfa_challenge` row → the provider session returned only in the ephemeral result. `completeStepUpWithTotp` takes `providerUserRef` + the ephemeral `providerChallenge` and answers the provider challenge outside any transaction; the `step_up_grant` is created only after Cognito confirms the actual MFA challenge; provider failures (invalid code, dead/replayed session, outage) create no local assurance. Himma `mfa_challenge` stays non-secret bookkeeping only; recovery-code step-up is untouched (no provider involvement).
- **Fake** ([fake-mfa-provider.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/providers/fake/fake-mfa-provider.ts)): mirrors the semantics — single-use, user-bound deterministic challenge sessions, plus a `callLog` so tests prove operation separation.

**Tests (rewritten provider-contract 7 + step-up 10; enrollment/recovery suites untouched):** enrollment uses ONLY Associate/VerifySoftwareToken and step-up NEVER calls the enrollment-verification operation (spy counters on the mock client AND the fake's call log through full service flows) · non-SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA or session-less challenge starts refused · invalid, expired, and REPLAYED provider sessions (and forged ones) create no grant · successful completion creates exactly one session-bound grant (Himma CAS still the deciding guard under concurrent replay with two valid provider sessions) · provider session material swept absent from every public table, audit, and outbox · normalized error objects carry nothing from provider exceptions (secret- and session-bearing messages) · recovery-code behavior unchanged. **Verification (2026-08-07): tsc clean · ESLint clean · full backend Jest 365/365 (33 suites) · no schema/codegen/frontend change, so no migration/codegen/native re-verification applies.**

**First-login race fix (`dd17030`, separate commit — APPROVED):** the B2-6B regression gate exposed a pre-existing B2-2-era defect — the spine concurrency test failed ~50% of runs at approved HEAD `0756456` (proven by 15-run loops before/after stash). Root cause, confirmed by captured divergence: under READ COMMITTED an identical concurrent first login committing between the loser's issuer+subject lookup and its verified-email pre-check (or its exclusion-constraint check) was misreported as `verifiedEmailConflict`, refusing a legitimate sign-in. Fix: both paths re-resolve the same-provider identity before declaring a conflict; genuine cross-user conflicts unchanged (49/49 conflict-suite tests green); spine suite 15/15 consecutive green after the fix. No merge behavior introduced.

**Verification (all green, 2026-08-07):** backend tsc clean · ESLint `--max-warnings=0` clean · backend Jest **360/360 across 37 suites, run twice** (36 new: provider contract 5 · enrollment 9 · recovery codes 11 · step-up 9 + hygiene assertions throughout; TDD red→green) · migrate-from-zero (4 migrations, none new) · `db:verify` green · codegen zero drift · all B2-1..B2-6A identity/session/HTTP/admin/bootstrap/MFA-schema suites green. **Live-Cognito items still pending under §14.E′:** real-pool smoke for AssociateSoftwareToken/VerifySoftwareToken mediation, the SDK-backed `CognitoMfaClient` instantiation, and any pool MFA configuration — all blocked on the separately-approved non-production pool; no live Cognito MFA validation is claimed.

## Backend Slice 2 · B2-6C (2026-08-07 — MFA routes, admin MFA enforcement, production capability gate, Slice 2 closeout; this commit, awaiting owner review)

**Routes ([mfa-routes.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/mfa-routes.ts), all TypeBox-schema'd, body-limited, rate-limited per user, `Cache-Control: no-store` via the B2-4 pipeline, stable sanitized envelopes, bearer never echoed):** `POST /auth/mfa/totp/enroll` (**stepUpRequired**) → `enrollmentStarted` with the ephemeral TOTP secret (schema-documented one-time; never stored/logged/audited/outboxed; no QR generation) · `POST /auth/mfa/totp/confirm` (**stepUpRequired**) → `mfaEnrolled` (+`supersededMethodId`); enrollmentInvalid/invalidCode/challengeExpired/invalidProviderState collapse into ONE `challengeInvalid` envelope · `POST /auth/mfa/recovery-codes/regenerate` (**stepUpRequired** — recent step-up enforced by policy) → raw codes exactly once with `presentation: 'one-time'`; no read path can ever return them · `POST /auth/step-up/totp/begin` (**authenticatedCustomer**) → non-secret Himma challenge bookkeeping + the ephemeral provider challenge session (49da921 semantics preserved: genuine SOFTWARE_TOKEN_MFA flow, never the enrollment verifier) · `POST /auth/step-up/totp/complete` → exactly one session-bound grant on provider-confirmed success; `tooManyAttempts → rateLimited`; failures grant nothing · `POST /auth/step-up/recovery-code` → Himma-owned atomic single-use consumption + same-transaction grant; wrong/consumed/superseded/never-existed byte-identical. These are the docs/26 §10 route set (the two-phase step-up is the begin/complete equivalent of `POST /auth/step-up`); no additional product flows invented. New outcome `challengeInvalid` (400) added to the envelope registry; new per-user rate-limit rules `mfaEnrollment`/`mfaChallenge`/`recoveryCode` (§14.C-configurable defaults).

**Principal assurance ([auth-plugin.ts](backend/src/modules/identity/http/auth-plugin.ts)):** `stepUpRequired` policy now accepts a fresh provider authentication (`auth_time` within the configured max-age) OR a live session-bound Himma `step_up_grant` (B2-6B `resolveSessionMfaAssurance`); the principal exposes safe derived `mfaEnrolled` and `stepUp {at, method}` only. Enrollment state alone is never recent assurance; grants are queried only on the already-liveness-checked session, so revoked/expired sessions void every grant (proven).

**Administrative enforcement (binding order, docs/23 §7):** live Himma session → **≥1 active Himma database role** (non-admins get plain `forbidden` and learn nothing about MFA) → **Himma MFA enrollment** (`mfaRequired` otherwise — a forged/asserted `amr: mfa` token cannot compensate) → **recent MFA-verified assurance**: an MFA login within the step-up window OR a live TOTP/recovery-code grant (`password`/`oidc` grants never satisfy admin MFA); MFA-verified-but-stale → `stepUpRequired`. Roles and assurance resolve fresh per request — role or session revocation bites regardless of any existing grant; Cognito groups/claims still grant nothing.

**Production capability gate ([build-app.ts](backend/src/app/build-app.ts)):** the temporary B2-5 placeholder is replaced by `AdminProductionReadiness` — production registers the admin surface ONLY when `cognitoConfigured` + `mfaProviderValidated` + `realPoolSmokeVerified` + `productionConfigApproved` ALL report true; otherwise absent (fail-closed 404), and explicitly forcing it in an unready production build refuses startup loudly. No default-true, no env shortcut, no test bypass — dev/test exercise the full behavior through deterministic adapters. Because the §14.E′ smoke has not run, **no truthful production configuration can report ready: Slice 2 implementation complete, production Cognito/admin-MFA activation pending operational validation** (recorded in docs/26 §15).

**Slice-2 hardening review (bounded closeout, all fifteen areas):** raw credential/token/secret persistence (schema guards + whole-DB sweeps) · token-type confusion (ID-token-as-bearer refused) · issuer/subject consistency (composite FKs + spine suite) · account merging (exclusion constraint; `dd17030` convergence) · session replay (one live session per `origin_jti`; §9.5 revocation) · logout/revocation (immediate liveness denial) · role escalation (DB-only roles; customer→admin `forbidden`) · dual control (CHECK + triggers) · bootstrap bypass (seal + structural single-role) · MFA bypass (two-way mirror triggers; enforcement order; fail-closed production gate) · recovery-code replay (CAS + write-once) · challenge replay (Himma CAS + single-use provider sessions) · missing route policy (structural onRoute guard + snapshot) · sensitive logging (zero logging in services/providers/MFA routes; throws carry key names/versions only) · audit/outbox leakage (payload sweeps). **No new confirmed Slice-2 security defect.** One behavioral alignment applied during implementation (admin-gate check order made exactly the binding order). Recorded future items, NOT expanded into scope: log-redaction policy for the future production server entrypoint · grant/challenge invalidation on MFA reset when the support-tooling disable flow lands (W3) · admin-portal `client_kind` sessions (Slice 3) · optional recovery-code remaining-count metadata read.

**Verification (full Slice-2 certification, 2026-08-07):** backend tsc clean · ESLint clean · backend Jest **390/390 across 34 suites** (25 new in [mfa-routes.test.ts](backend/test/mfa-routes.test.ts): policy/structure/schema-rejection · enrollment ephemeral material + provider-gated activation · recovery codes once-only + regeneration + recent-step-up requirement · TOTP step-up ephemeral session, single grant, invalid/replayed/expired refusals, attempt throttling · recovery-code step-up single-use + concurrency + indistinguishable failures · expired/invalidated-grant and revoked-session assurance boundaries · admin enforcement matrix incl. non-MFA-grant refusal and unenrolled-admin refusal · production gate closed/partial/forced/ready · whole-DB secret sweep) · migrate-from-zero (4 migrations, none new) · `db:verify` green · codegen zero drift · route-policy inventory snapshot updated intentionally (+6 MFA routes) · root customer app untouched: tsc clean · expo lint clean · Jest **421/421 across 24 suites**.

**Slice 3 entry criteria (do not start without ALL):** explicit owner approval of B2-6C and the Slice-2 closeout — **✅ granted 2026-08-07 (`f95b84a` approved, slice closed)** · an owner-approved Slice-3 specification — **drafted as docs/27 (this commit), awaiting owner review** · the docs/23 §16 phase gate satisfied · production identity activation items remain independent operational work (§14.E′ smoke, docs/23 §18.3 region/DR, production config) and do NOT block Slice-3 planning, but real-pool validation must precede any production admin enablement.

## Backend Slice 3 · S3-1 (2026-08-07 — organization/profile/branch schema foundation; **FULLY APPROVED at `3b5d12f` 2026-08-07**)

**Migration [`0005_provider_organizations.sql`](backend/migrations/0005_provider_organizations.sql)** (one transaction, reviewed Down, all Slice-1 conventions) — exactly the docs/27 §2–§4/§12 S3-1 scope; no staff, invitation, route, listing, or portal object exists.

- **`organization`** — canonical PRIVATE commercial counterparty: `legal_name` (never public) vs `trade_name`, `org_kind` CHECK `'provider'` (D-S3-4 seam), `origin` CHECK `'admin_created'` (D-S3-2 seam; self-signup widens additively), `verification_state` with the docs/24 §5.1 machine enforced by `enforce_organization_transition` (every edge verbatim incl. rejected→submitted resubmission and suspended⇄live; invalid skips refused at trigger level even if a future service forgets; `offboarded` terminal — the whole row freezes), state-tied `suspended_at`/`offboarded_at` CHECKs (reinstatement must clear the timestamp), `commercial_terms_ref` (structure only, §14.B4 open), identity/provenance immutability, partial index on `live`. Actor metadata is audit-trail data, not columns. NO bank/payout/licence-content/commission/listing field exists anywhere in the slice.
- **`organization_public_profile`** — the 1:1 customer-facing storefront record (PK = organization_id, re-pointing refused by trigger): display name, en/ar descriptions, logo/cover/gallery media refs, four explicit opt-in public contact fields, and `published` (A1). Structural separation: future customer reads select from THIS table + public branch columns only; no json/jsonb column exists on it (nothing can smuggle private data in — schema-tested). **Documented semantics: `published` is publication intent/readiness and MAY be set pre-live (drafting allowed, no restricting trigger per the A1 ruling); EFFECTIVE visibility is always `verification_state='live' AND published`, and suspension/offboarding removes availability via the lifecycle regardless of the flag** — the predicate itself is exercised in tests across draft→live→suspended.
- **`branch`** — organization-owned location: label, address/city, `area_id` (FK-less until the Slice-4 taxonomy migration; `area_label` serves reads meanwhile), `geo_point` (lng,lat)-ordered with range CHECKs, `opening_hours` jsonb, `facilities text[]`, `active` (deactivate-only; no DELETE grant), ownership immutable by trigger, `ix_branch_organization (organization_id, active)`, and the binding **`UNIQUE (id, organization_id)` composite ownership target**.
- **Future-listing compatibility proven structurally:** a scratch probe of the docs/24 §1.4 Program shape (`listing.organization_id` FK + a listing↔branch join composite-FK'd to `branch (id, organization_id)`) attaches with zero alteration to the three S3-1 tables, and a cross-organization branch association is refused by the composite FK; the probe is dropped in-test — S3-1 ships no listing/category/membership/package table (asserted).
- **Grants:** `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE` on all three tables to `himma_app`, no DELETE (tested). Audit/outbox: no new tables — the migration header records the org.* / organization.* event vocabulary the later service commits emit through the Slice-1 machinery.
